\displaystyle N_{x}\mathbb{E}_{\mathscr{X}\sim\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}}\left[B_{U_{\lambda}}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}},\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})\right]+N_{x}\mathbb{E}_{\mathscr{X}\sim\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}}\left[U_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}))\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-N_{x}\cdot U_{\lambda}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))+2\lambda\left(R+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\sigma_{1}}}R^{2}\right).

(57)

The second last inequality Eq. (
56
) holds because of the following upper bound on the difference: for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,N_{x}\}
,

\displaystyle\quad\left|\delta_{\mu_{x}}U_{\lambda}\left(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}})\right)(x^{(i)})-\delta_{\mu_{x}}U_{\lambda}\left(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}\;,\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}})\right)(x^{(i)})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\left|\delta_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}}))(x^{(i)})-\delta_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}))(x^{(i)})\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\lambda\left|\delta_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}}))(x^{(i)})-\delta_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}))(x^{(i)})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\lambda}{N_{x}}\left(R+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\sigma_{1}}}R^{2}\right),

where the first inequality holds from the expression the first variation of
U_{\lambda}
derived in Eq. (
53
), and the second inequality holds by using

10.3
.
To proceed from Eq. (
57
) above, we can keep obtaining a lower bound using the lower bound on the Bregman divergence
B_{U_{\lambda}}
proved in
5.8
.

(
57
)

\displaystyle\geq N_{x}\mathbb{E}_{\mathscr{X}\sim\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}}\left[U_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}))\right]-N_{x}\cdot U_{\lambda}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{N_{x}R\lambda}{4\sigma_{1}}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\mathscr{X}\sim\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\left(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}-\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}\right)\right)^{2}\right]\right]+2\lambda\left(R+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\sigma_{1}}}R^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq N_{x}\mathbb{E}_{\mathscr{X}\sim\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}}\left[U_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}))\right]-N_{x}\cdot U_{\lambda}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{N_{x}R^{3}\lambda}{4\sigma_{1}}\cdot\left(8\sqrt{N_{x}^{-1}\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})},(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})^{\otimes N_{x}})}+4N_{x}^{-1}\right)+2\lambda\left(R+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\sigma_{1}}}R^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq N_{x}\mathbb{E}_{\mathscr{X}\sim\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}}\left[U_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}))\right]-N_{x}\cdot U_{\lambda}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{N_{x}R^{3}\lambda}{4\sigma_{1}}\cdot\left(8\mathfrak{c}^{-1}N_{x}^{-1}\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})},(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})^{\otimes N_{x}})+2\mathfrak{c}+4N_{x}^{-1}\right)+2\lambda\left(R+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\sigma_{1}}}R^{2}\right).

The second last inequality holds by using Proposition 1 of
57
, and the last inequality holds by

\displaystyle\sqrt{N_{x}^{-1}\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})},(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})^{\otimes N_{x}})}\leq\mathfrak{c}^{-1}N_{x}^{-1}\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})},(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})^{\otimes N_{x}})+\frac{1}{4}\mathfrak{c}.

Now we have successfully obtained a lower bound of the first term in Eq. (
55
), next we are about to lower bound the second term in Eq. (
55
).

Take the expectation of the second term in Eq. (
55
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathscr{X}\sim\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}}\left[\mathrm{KL}\left(P_{x^{(i)}\mid\mathscr{X}_{-i}},\nu\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}}\left[\operatorname{Ent}\left(P_{x^{(i)}\mid\mathscr{X}_{-i}}\right)\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\log\left(\nu(x^{(i)})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\operatorname{Ent}\left(\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}\right)-\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\log\left(\nu(x^{(i)})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{KL}\left(\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})},\nu^{\otimes N_{x}}\right),

where the inequality hold by using Lemma 3.6 of
13
.
Combing all the above together results in a lower bound of Eq. (
55
). If we plug it back to Eq. (
51
) and then back to Eq. (
49
), then we obtain

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{N_{x}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}}\left[\left\|\nabla_{i}\log\left(\frac{\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}}{\mu_{\ast}^{(N_{x})}}(\mathscr{X})\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{2C_{\mathrm{LSI},x}}{\sigma_{2}N_{x}}\left(N_{x}\mathbb{E}_{\mathscr{X}\sim\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}}\left[U_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}))-U_{\lambda}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))\right]\right.

\displaystyle\quad+\left.\sigma_{2}\mathrm{KL}\left(\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})},\nu^{\otimes N_{x}}\right)-N_{x}\sigma_{2}\mathrm{KL}\left(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\nu\right)\right)+\frac{2C_{\mathrm{LSI},x}\lambda}{\sigma_{2}N_{x}}\left(R^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\sigma_{1}}}R^{3}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{C_{\mathrm{LSI},x}}{\sigma_{2}N_{x}}\frac{R^{3}\lambda}{\sigma_{1}}\cdot\left(2\mathfrak{c}^{-1}\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})},(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})^{\otimes N_{x}})+\frac{\mathfrak{c}}{2}N_{x}+1\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2C_{\mathrm{LSI},x}}{\sigma_{2}N_{x}}\left(\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}^{(N_{x})}\left(\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}\right)-N_{x}\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{*}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}),\mu_{z}^{*}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))\right)+\frac{2\lambda C_{\mathrm{LSI},x}\left(R^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\sigma_{1}}}R^{3}\right)}{\sigma_{2}N_{x}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{R^{3}\lambda C_{\mathrm{LSI},x}}{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}N_{x}}\cdot\left(2\mathfrak{c}^{-1}\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})},(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})^{\otimes N_{x}})+\frac{\mathfrak{c}}{2}N_{x}+1\right).

(58)

Remark 9.1

(Comparison with the defective LSI from standard mean field Langevin dynamics)
.

We compare Eq. (
58
) with the defective LSI commonly used in the analysis of standard mean field Langevin dynamics with a convex objective functional (e.g., Lemma 1 of
57
). The first two terms on the right-hand side of Eq. (
58
) represent the descent term and the finite particle approximation error term, which align with the corresponding terms in Lemma 1 of
57
. However, our analysis includes an additional positive third term, which arises due to the
non-convexity
of the objective functional
U_{\lambda}
and
L_{\lambda}
.

To proceed from Eq. (
58
), we next upper bound
N_{x}^{-1}\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})},(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})^{\otimes N_{x}})
with
5.9
.

\displaystyle N_{x}^{-1}\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})},(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})^{\otimes N_{x}})\leq\frac{2}{\sigma_{2}}\left(N_{x}^{-1}\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}^{(N_{x})}(\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})})-\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))\right)+\frac{4R^{3}\lambda}{N_{x}\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}+\frac{4R^{3}\lambda\mathfrak{c}}{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}.

Therefore, plugging the above back to Eq. (
58
), we have

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{N_{x}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}}\left[\left\|\nabla_{i}\log\left(\frac{\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}}{\mu_{\ast}^{(N_{x})}}(\mathscr{X})\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{2C_{\mathrm{LSI},x}}{\sigma_{2}N_{x}}\left(\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}^{(N_{x})}\left(\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}\right)-N_{x}\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{*}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}),\mu_{z}^{*}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))\right)+\frac{\lambda C_{\mathrm{LSI},x}R^{2}\left(\frac{R}{\sigma_{1}}+2+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\sigma_{1}}}R\right)}{\sigma_{2}N_{x}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{C_{\mathrm{LSI},x}}{\sigma_{2}N_{x}}\left(\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}^{(N_{x})}\left(\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}\right)-N_{x}\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{*}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}),\mu_{z}^{*}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))\right)+\frac{\mathfrak{c}^{2}C_{\mathrm{LSI},x}}{8}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{C_{\mathrm{LSI},x}}{\sigma_{2}N_{x}}\left(\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}^{(N_{x})}\left(\mu_{x}^{(N_{x})}\right)-N_{x}\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{*}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}),\mu_{z}^{*}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))\right)+\frac{\lambda C_{\mathrm{LSI},x}R^{2}\left(\frac{R}{\sigma_{1}}+2+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\sigma_{1}}}R\right)}{\sigma_{2}N_{x}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\mathfrak{c}^{2}C_{\mathrm{LSI},x}}{8}.

The proof is concluded by taking expectation with respect to
\mathbb{E}_{\mathscr{Z}\sim\mu_{z,s}^{(N_{z})},\;\tilde{\mathscr{Z}}\sim\tilde{\mu}_{z,s}^{(N_{z})}}
on both sides.
∎

Lemma 9.2
.

Suppose
2
holds. Let
L_{\lambda}
be as defined in (
20
) where
L_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z},\mu_{z})=F_{2}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z})+\lambda\cdot F_{1}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z})+\lambda\sigma_{1}\cdot\mathrm{Ent}(\tilde{\mu}_{z})-\lambda\cdot F_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})-\lambda\sigma_{1}\cdot\mathrm{Ent}(\mu_{z})
.
Consider the following particle system:
for
i=1,\ldots,N_{x}
and
0<\tau\leq\gamma
,

\displaystyle\hat{x}_{\tau}^{(i)}=\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}-\tau\cdot\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{\mathscr{Z},s},\mu_{\mathscr{Z},s})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})+\sqrt{2\sigma_{2}\tau}\xi_{x}^{(i)},

(59)

where
\{\xi_{x}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{N_{x}}
are
N_{x}
i.i.d unit normal random variables. Then, for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},\tau},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},\tau}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},\tau}))(x)-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},\tau},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0}))(x)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda^{2}R^{6}}{\sigma_{1}}\left(\tau^{2}\left(\zeta_{2}^{2}\frac{1}{N_{x}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}\|^{2}]+\lambda^{2}R^{2}\right)+\tau\sigma_{2}d_{x}\right).

Proof.

From the definition of
L_{\lambda}
, we have the following relation on its Wasserstein gradient
\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{\mathscr{Z},s},\mu_{\mathscr{Z},s})
:

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau}))(x)-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0}))(x)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\zeta_{2}x+\lambda\cdot\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau}))(x)-\lambda\cdot\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau}))(x)

\displaystyle\qquad-\zeta_{2}x+\lambda\cdot\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0}))(x)+\lambda\cdot\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0}))(x)\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau}))(x)-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0}))(x)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\lambda\left\|\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau}))(x)-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0}))(x)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{2}R^{4}\cdot\Big(\mathrm{TV}^{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau}),\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0}))+\mathrm{TV}^{2}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0}))\Big).

(60)

The last inequality holds from the Lipschitzness of
\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}
proved in
10.5
.
Next, from the Pinsker’s inequality and
10.4
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}^{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0}),\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau}))\leq 2\mathrm{KL}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0}),\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau}))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{R^{2}}{2\sigma_{1}}\left[W_{2}^{2}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau})\right].

From the update scheme in Eq. (
59
), we have

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\mathscr{X}}_{0}\sim\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0},\;\hat{\mathscr{X}}_{\tau}\sim\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},\tau}}\left[W_{2}^{2}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N_{x}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\mathscr{X}}_{0}\sim\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0},\;\hat{\mathscr{X}}_{\tau}\sim\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},\tau}}\left[\|\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}-\hat{x}_{\tau}^{(i)}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{x}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\tau\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{\mathscr{Z},s},\mu_{\mathscr{Z},s})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})+\sqrt{2\sigma_{2}\tau}\xi_{x}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{x}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\tau^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{\mathscr{Z},s},\mu_{\mathscr{Z},s})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})\right\|^{2}\right]+2\tau\sigma_{2}d_{x}.

(61)

For
i=1,\ldots,N_{x}
,

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{\mathscr{Z},s},\mu_{\mathscr{Z},s})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\zeta_{2}\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}+\lambda\cdot\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\mu_{\mathscr{Z},s})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})-\lambda\cdot\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{\mathscr{Z},s})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\zeta_{2}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}\|^{2}]+\lambda^{2}R^{2}.

The last inequality holds from the fact that
\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}
is bounded as per
2
.
Combined, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}^{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0}),\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau}))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{R^{2}}{2\sigma_{1}}W_{2}^{2}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{R^{2}}{\sigma_{1}}\left(\tau^{2}\left(\zeta_{2}^{2}\frac{1}{N_{x}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}\|^{2}]+\lambda^{2}R^{2}\right)+\tau\sigma_{2}d_{x}\right).

(62)

Similarly, we also have

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}^{2}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau}))\lesssim\frac{R^{2}}{\sigma_{1}}\left(\tau^{2}\left(\zeta_{2}^{2}\frac{1}{N_{x}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}\|^{2}]+\lambda^{2}R^{2}\right)+\tau\sigma_{2}d_{x}\right).

(63)

We plug the upper bound in Eq. (
9.2.4
) and Eq. (
63
) back to Eq. (
9.2.4
), and we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau}))(x)-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0}))(x)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\lambda^{2}R^{6}}{\sigma_{1}}\left(\tau^{2}\left(\zeta_{2}^{2}\frac{1}{N_{x}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}\|^{2}]+\lambda^{2}R^{2}\right)+\tau\sigma_{2}d_{x}\right).

∎

Lemma 9.3
.

Suppose
2
holds. Let
L_{\lambda}
be as defined in Eq. (
20
) where
L_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z},\mu_{z})=F_{2}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z})+\lambda\cdot F_{1}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z})+\lambda\sigma_{1}\cdot\mathrm{Ent}(\tilde{\mu}_{z})-\lambda\cdot F_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})-\lambda\sigma_{1}\cdot\mathrm{Ent}(\mu_{z})
.
Consider the following particle system:
for
i=1,\ldots,N_{x}
and
0<\tau\leq\gamma
,

\displaystyle\hat{x}_{\tau}^{(i)}=\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}-\tau\cdot\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{\mathscr{Z},s},\mu_{\mathscr{Z},s})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})+\sqrt{2\sigma_{2}\tau}\xi_{x}^{(i)},

(64)

where
\{\xi_{x}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{N_{x}}
are
N_{x}
i.i.d unit normal random variables. Then, for any fixed
\tilde{\mu}_{z},\mu_{z}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}})
,

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{z},\mu_{z})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},\tau},\tilde{\mu}_{z},\mu_{z})(\hat{x}_{\tau}^{(i)})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim(2\lambda^{2}R^{4}+\zeta_{2}^{2})\left(\tau^{2}\left(\zeta_{2}^{2}\frac{1}{N_{x}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}\|^{2}]+\lambda^{2}R^{2}\right)+2\tau\sigma_{2}d_{x}\right).

(65)

Here, the expectation is taken over the joint distribution of the particles
\{\hat{x}_{\tau}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\sim\hat{\mu}_{x,\tau}^{(N_{x})}
and
\{\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\sim\hat{\mu}_{x,0}^{(N_{x})}
.

Proof.

From the definition of
L_{\lambda}
in Eq. (
20
) and its Wasserstein gradient in Eq. (
4.2
), we have

\displaystyle\quad\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{z},\mu_{z})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}F_{2}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{z})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})+\lambda\cdot\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}F_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{z})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})-\lambda\cdot\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}F_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\mu_{z})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})

\displaystyle=\zeta_{2}\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}+\lambda\cdot\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{z})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})-\lambda\cdot\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\mu_{z})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}).

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\quad\left\|\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{z},\mu_{z})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau},\tilde{\mu}_{z},\mu_{z})(\hat{x}_{\tau}^{(i)})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\zeta_{2}^{2}\|\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}-\hat{x}_{\tau}^{(i)}\|^{2}+\lambda^{2}\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{z})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau},\tilde{\mu}_{z})(\hat{x}_{\tau}^{(i)})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\lambda^{2}\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{z})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau},\tilde{\mu}_{z})(\hat{x}_{\tau}^{(i)})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\lambda^{2}R^{4}\left(W_{2}^{2}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau})+\|\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}-\hat{x}_{\tau}^{(i)}\|^{2}\right)+\zeta_{2}^{2}\|\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}-\hat{x}_{\tau}^{(i)}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\lambda^{2}R^{4}\left(\frac{1}{N_{x}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\|\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}-\hat{x}_{\tau}^{(i)}\|^{2}+\|\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}-\hat{x}_{\tau}^{(i)}\|^{2}\right)+\zeta_{2}^{2}\|\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}-\hat{x}_{\tau}^{(i)}\|^{2}.

Here, the second last inequality holds from the Lipschitz continuity of Wasserstein gradients
\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}
proved in
10.5
, and the last inequality holds by the definition of Wasserstein distance.
From the update scheme and the same derivations as done in Eq. (
61
), we have

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}_{\{\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\sim\hat{\mu}_{x,0}^{(N_{x})}\;\{\hat{x}_{\tau}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\sim\hat{\mu}_{x,\tau}^{(N_{x})}}\left[\|\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}-\hat{x}_{\tau}^{(i)}\|^{2}\right]\leq\tau^{2}\left(\zeta_{2}^{2}\frac{1}{N_{x}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}\|^{2}]+\lambda^{2}R^{2}\right)+2\tau\sigma_{2}d_{x}.

The left hand side of Eq. (
9.3
) can be upper bounded by

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{z},\mu_{z})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},\tau},\tilde{\mu}_{z},\mu_{z})(\hat{x}_{\tau}^{(i)})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim(2\lambda^{2}R^{4}+\zeta_{2}^{2})\left(\tau^{2}\left(\zeta_{2}^{2}\frac{1}{N_{x}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}\|^{2}]+\lambda^{2}R^{2}\right)+2\tau\sigma_{2}d_{x}\right).

The proof is thus concluded.
∎

Lemma 9.4
.

Suppose
2
holds. Let
L_{\lambda}
be as defined in Eq. (
20
) where
L_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z},\mu_{z})=F_{2}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z})+\lambda\cdot F_{1}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z})+\lambda\sigma_{1}\cdot\mathrm{Ent}(\tilde{\mu}_{z})-\lambda\cdot F_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})-\lambda\sigma_{1}\cdot\mathrm{Ent}(\mu_{z})
.
Consider the following particle system:
for
i=1,\ldots,N_{x}
and
0<\tau\leq\gamma
,

\displaystyle\hat{x}_{\tau}^{(i)}=\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)}-\tau\cdot\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{\mathscr{Z}},\mu_{\mathscr{Z}})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})+\sqrt{2\sigma_{2}\tau}\xi_{x}^{(i)},

(66)

where
\{\xi_{x}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{N_{x}}
are
N_{x}
i.i.d unit normal random variables, and

\tilde{\mu}_{\mathscr{Z}}=\frac{1}{N_{z}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{z}}\delta_{z},\mu_{\mathscr{Z}}=\frac{1}{N_{z}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{z}}\delta_{\tilde{z}}
are the output of the inner-loop algorithm
InnerLoop
(
\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0}
,
T
,
\alpha
,
\beta
,
\lambda
,
\sigma_{1}
) detailed in
Algorithm

1
. Denote
\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{(N_{z})},\mu_{z}^{(N_{z})}
as the joint distribution of the corresponding
N_{z}
particles.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0}))(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{\mathscr{Z}},\mu_{\mathscr{Z}})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{2}R^{4}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{KL}\left(\mu_{z}^{(N_{z})},{(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0}))}^{\otimes N_{z}}\right)}{N_{z}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{KL}\left(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{(N_{z})},{(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0}))}^{\otimes N_{z}}\right)}{N_{z}}}+1\right).

(67)

Here, the expectation is taken over the joint distribution of the particles
\{\tilde{z}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{N_{z}}\sim\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{(N_{z})}
and
\{z^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{N_{z}}\sim\mu_{z}^{(N_{z})}
.

Proof.

From the definition of
L_{\lambda}
in Eq. (
20
) and its Wasserstein gradient in Eq. (
4.2
), we have

\displaystyle\quad\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0}),\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0}))(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}L_{\lambda}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{\mathscr{Z}},\mu_{\mathscr{Z}})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}F_{2}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0}))(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})+\lambda\cdot\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}F_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0}))(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})-\lambda\cdot\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}F_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0}))(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})

\displaystyle\qquad-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}F_{2}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{\mathscr{Z}})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})-\lambda\cdot\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}F_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\mu_{\mathscr{Z}})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})+\lambda\cdot\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}F_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{\mathscr{Z}})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})

\displaystyle=\lambda\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0}))(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\mu_{\mathscr{Z}})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\lambda\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0}))(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\tilde{\mu}_{\mathscr{Z}})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})\right).

(68)

From the Lipschitz continuity of the Wasserstein gradients
\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}
proved in
10.5
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0}))(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\mu_{\mathscr{Z}})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})\right\|\leq R\left|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;(\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathscr{Z}}-\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0}))\right]\right|.

Next, from
57
, we obtain

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}_{\{z^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{N_{z}}\sim\mu_{z}^{(N_{z})}}\left[\left\|\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0}))(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X},0},\mu_{\mathscr{Z}})(\hat{x}_{0}^{(i)})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq R^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\{z^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{N_{z}}\sim\mu_{z}^{(N_{z})}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;(\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathscr{Z}}-\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0}))\right]\right)^{2}\leq 8R^{4}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{KL}\left(\mu_{z}^{(N_{z})},{(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathscr{X},0}))}^{\otimes N_{z}}\right)}{N_{z}}}+\frac{4R^{4}}{N_{z}}.

Similarly, we can do the same for the second term in Eq. (
68
), which concludes the proof.
∎

Proposition 9.5

(Partial-convexity)
.

Let
U_{\lambda}:\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}})\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}})
to
\mathbb{R}
be a mapping as defined in Eq. (
52
). For fixed
\tilde{\mu}_{z},\mu_{z}
, the mapping
\mu_{x}\mapsto U_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z},\mu_{z})
is convex.

Proof.

Notice that

\displaystyle\quad U_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z},\mu_{z})=U_{2}(\mu_{z})+\lambda\cdot F_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})+\sigma_{1}\mathrm{Ent}(\mu_{z})-\lambda\cdot F_{1}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z})-\lambda\sigma_{1}\mathrm{Ent}(\tilde{\mu}_{z})

\displaystyle=\lambda\cdot\left(U_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})-U_{1}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z})\right)+\text{const}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda}{2}\cdot\left(\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}\right)^{2}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}\right)^{2}\right]\right)+\text{const}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda}{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(2\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}\right)\cdot\text{const}\right]+\text{const}.

Here, the const are constants that are independent of
\mu_{x}
. Hence, we can see that the partial mapping
\mu_{x}\mapsto U_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z},\mu_{z})
is a linear mapping and hence convex.
∎

9.3
Proofs in
Section

6

Proposition 9.6

(Stage I generalization bound)
.

Suppose
1
,
2
and
4
hold.
For any
\mu_{x}
that satisfies
\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x},\nu_{x})\leq\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x}^{\circ},\nu_{x})+2\sigma_{2}^{-1}R^{2}
, let
\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})=\arg\min_{\mu_{z}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}})}\mathscr{F}_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})
be the optimal solution to Stage I. Then, with
P^{\otimes m}
probability at least
1-2\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P_{W}}\left[\Big(\smallint\Psi(W,z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi(W,z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x})\Big)^{2}\right]\lesssim\sigma_{1}M_{z}+R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{m}}+R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{M_{z}+\sigma_{1}^{-1}R^{2}}{m}}.

Proof of
9.6
.

From the optimality of
\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Big(\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{a}_{i},x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}-\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\Big)^{2}+\sigma_{1}\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}),\nu_{z})

\displaystyle\leq\mathscr{F}_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Big(\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{a}_{i},x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}-\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x})\Big)^{2}+\sigma_{1}\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x}),\nu_{z}).

(69)

For each
i
, denote
\Upsilon(\mathbf{a}_{i},\mathbf{w}_{i})=\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{a}_{i},x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}-\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x})
which is zero mean by definition of
\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x})
and it is also subgaussian as it is bounded by
2
.
From Corollary 2.8.3 in
78
, with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Upsilon(\mathbf{a}_{i},\mathbf{w}_{i})^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}_{P_{AW}}[\Upsilon(A,W)^{2}]+R^{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{m}}+\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{m}\right).

Here,
\mathbb{E}_{P_{AW}}[\Upsilon(A,W)^{2}]
represents the Bayes optimal risk in stage I regression.
For sufficiently large
m
, we have

(
69
)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P_{AW}}[\Upsilon(A,W)^{2}]+R^{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{m}}+\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{m}\right)+\sigma_{1}\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x}),\nu_{z})

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}_{P_{AW}}[\Upsilon(A,W)^{2}]+\sigma_{1}M_{z}.

Hence, we have
\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}),\nu_{z})\leq 2\sigma_{1}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{P_{AW}}[\Upsilon(A,W)^{2}]+M_{z}
.
Denote

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{B}}(M_{z}):=\left\{(\mathbf{a},\mathbf{w})\mapsto\Big(f(\mathbf{w})-\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{a},x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}\Big)^{2}\Big|\quad f:\mathcal{W}\to\mathbb{R}\in\mathcal{B}_{M_{z}}\right\}.

Definition 5

(Empirical Rademacher complexity)
.

The empirical Rademacher complexity of a function class
\mathcal{F}
of functions
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{R}
is defined as
\mathfrak{R}(\mathcal{F}):=\mathbb{E}_{\sigma}[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}f_{t}(x_{i})]
, where
\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are
n
i.i.d Rademacher random variables.

Since
\Psi
is bounded from
2
, by Talagrand’s contraction
lemma
(
55
, Lemma 5.7)
, we obtain that
\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\mathcal{B}}(M_{z}+2\sigma_{1}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{P_{AW}}[\Upsilon(A,W)^{2}]))\leq 2R\cdot\mathfrak{R}(\mathcal{B}(M_{z}+2\sigma_{1}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{P_{AW}}[\Upsilon(A,W)^{2}]))
.
Utilizing the standard uniform bound
(
80
)
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\quad\sup_{g\in\overline{\mathcal{B}}(M_{z}+4\sigma_{1}^{-1})}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{P_{AW}}[g(A,W)]-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}g(\mathbf{a}_{i},\mathbf{w}_{i})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\mathcal{B}}(M_{z}+2\sigma_{1}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{P_{AW}}[\Upsilon(A,W)^{2}]))+12R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{2m}}

\displaystyle\leq 4R\cdot\mathfrak{R}(\mathcal{B}(M_{z}+2\sigma_{1}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{P_{AW}}[\Upsilon(A,W)^{2}]))+12R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{2m}}

\displaystyle\leq 4R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{M_{z}+2\sigma_{1}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{P_{AW}}[\Upsilon(A,W)^{2}]}{m}}+12R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{2m}}.

The last inequality holds by using the upper bound on the empirical Rademacher complexity proved in
10.7
.
Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P_{AW}}\left[\Big(\smallint\Psi(W,z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi(A,x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}\Big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Big(\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{a}_{i},x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt+4R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{M_{z}+2\sigma_{1}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{P_{AW}}[\Upsilon(A,W)^{2}]}{m}}+12R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{2m}}

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt\leq\eqref{eq:optimization_gap}+4R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{M_{z}+2\sigma_{1}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{P_{AW}}[\Upsilon(A,W)^{2}]}{m}}+12R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{2m}}

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt\leq\mathbb{E}_{P_{AW}}[\Upsilon(A,W)^{2}]+R^{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{m}}+\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{m}\right)+\sigma_{1}M_{z}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt+4R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{M_{z}+2\sigma_{1}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{P_{AW}}[\Upsilon(A,W)^{2}]}{m}}+12R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{2m}}

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{P_{AW}}[\Upsilon(A,W)^{2}]+\sigma_{1}M_{z}+R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{m}}+R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{M_{z}+\sigma_{1}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{P_{AW}}[\Upsilon(A,W)^{2}]}{m}}.

(70)

Finally, notice that the left hand side of the above inequality equals

\displaystyle\text{LHS of }\eqref{eq:dummy_generalization_proof}=\mathbb{E}_{P_{W}}\left[\Big(\smallint\Psi(W,z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi(W,z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x})\Big)^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{P_{AW}}[\Upsilon(A,W)^{2}].

The proof is thus concluded.
∎

9.3.1
Proof of
Theorem

6.1

Proof of
Theorem

6.1
.

From
3
, there exists
\mu_{x}^{\circ}
such that
h_{\circ}(\mathbf{a})=\int\Psi(\mathbf{a},x)\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}^{\circ}
and
\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x}^{\circ},\nu_{x})\leq M_{x}
.
From the optimality of
\mu_{x}^{\ast}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x}^{\ast},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\ast}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\ast})-\mathbf{y}_{i}\Big)^{2}+\sigma_{2}\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x}^{\ast},\nu_{x})\leq\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x}^{\circ},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\circ})).

Next, from the second point of
Theorem

4.5
, we know
(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\mu_{z,\lambda}^{\ast})
, the global optimum of (

λ

-penalty
) with
\lambda>\lambda_{0}
, is also the global-minimum of (

ε

-constrained
) with
\varepsilon=\epsilon_{1}/(\lambda-\lambda_{0})
where
\epsilon_{1}=\frac{R^{2}(R+M)^{2}}{8\sigma_{1}\lambda_{0}}
.
From the third point of
Theorem

4.5
, we know that

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))-R(R+M)\sqrt{(2\sigma_{1})^{-1}\varepsilon}\leq\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\mu_{z,\lambda}^{\ast})\leq\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x}^{\ast},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\ast}))

If we take
\lambda_{0}=\frac{\lambda}{2}
, then we have
\varepsilon=\frac{R^{2}(R+M)^{2}}{2\sigma_{1}\lambda^{2}}
.
So we have obtained that

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))\leq\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x}^{\ast},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\ast}))+\frac{R^{2}(R+M)^{2}}{2\sigma_{1}\lambda}\leq\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x}^{\circ},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\circ}))+\frac{R^{2}(R+M)^{2}}{2\sigma_{1}\lambda}.

By adding and subtracting the same term on both sides, it gives

\displaystyle\quad\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))+\Big(\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))-\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x}^{\circ},\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x}^{\circ}))+\frac{R^{2}(R+M)^{2}}{2\sigma_{1}\lambda}+\Big(\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x}^{\circ},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\circ}))-\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x}^{\circ},\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x}^{\circ}))\Big).

(71)

Notice that

\displaystyle\quad\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x}^{\circ},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\circ}))-\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x}^{\circ},\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x}^{\circ}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\Big(\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\circ})-\mathbf{y}_{i}\Big)^{2}-\Big(\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x}^{\circ})-\mathbf{y}_{i}\Big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\Big(\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\circ})+\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x}^{\circ})-2\mathbf{y}_{i}\Big)\cdot\Big(\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\circ})-\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x}^{\circ})\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\circ})-\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x}^{\circ})\Big)^{2}+4RM\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{n}}.

(72)

The last inequality holds by that, for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,
\mathbf{y}_{i}-\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x}^{\circ})=\mathbf{y}_{i}-(Th_{\circ})(\mathbf{w}_{i})
is bounded and hence subgaussian as per
1
.
From the Bernstein’s concentration inequality along with the generalization bounded in
9.6
, with probability at least
1-2\delta
,

(
72
)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P_{W}}\left[\Big(\smallint\Psi(W,z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\circ})-\smallint\Psi(W,z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x}^{\circ})\Big)^{2}\right]+R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{n}}+4RM\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{n}}

\displaystyle:=(\dagger)+(R^{2}+4RM)\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{n}}.

Here,
(\dagger)
represents the generalization bounded in
9.6
.

Similarly, we can obtain

\displaystyle\quad\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))-\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})+\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})-2\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x}^{\circ})\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Big(\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})-\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})\Big)+4RM\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})-\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})\Big)^{2}+4RM\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{n}}

(73)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\;\mathrm{d}\left(\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})-\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x}^{\circ})\right)\Big)\cdot\Big(\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})-\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})\Big).

(74)

Notice that

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:scrF_2_bound_term_two}=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(T\left[\smallint\Psi(\cdot,x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}\right](\mathbf{w}_{i})-T\left[\smallint\Psi(\cdot,x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}^{\circ}\right](\mathbf{w}_{i})\Big)\cdot\Big(\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\;\mathrm{d}\left(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})-\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})\right)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(T\left[\smallint\Psi(\cdot,x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}\right](\mathbf{w}_{i})-(Th_{\circ})(\mathbf{w}_{i})\Big)^{2}+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})-\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})\Big)^{2}

From the Bernstein’s concentration inequality along with the generalization bound proved in
9.6
, with probability at least
1-2\delta
,

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:scrF_2_bound_term_one}+\eqref{eq:scrF_2_bound_term_two}

\displaystyle\leq 3\mathbb{E}_{P_{W}}\left[\Big(\smallint\Psi(W,z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})-\smallint\Psi(W,z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})\Big)^{2}\right]+(R^{2}+4RM)\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{n}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(T\left[\smallint\Psi(\cdot,x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}\right](\mathbf{w}_{i})-(Th_{\circ})(\mathbf{w}_{i})\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 3(\dagger)+(R^{2}+4RM)\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{n}}+\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(T\left[\smallint\Psi(\cdot,x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}\right](\mathbf{w}_{i})-(Th_{\circ})(\mathbf{w}_{i})\Big)^{2}.

Here,
(\dagger)
represents the generalization bounded in
9.6
.

Therefore, we are about to plug the above upper bound on
\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))-\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))
and the upper bound on
\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x}^{\circ},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\circ}))-\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x}^{\circ},\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x}^{\circ}))
back to Eq. (
71
). To simplify the expression, we use
\lesssim
to suppress the constants. We obtain, with probability at least
1-4\delta
,

\displaystyle\quad\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))\lesssim\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x}^{\circ},\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x}^{\circ}))+(\dagger)+\frac{R^{2}(R+M)^{2}}{\sigma_{1}\lambda}+(R^{2}+RM)\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{n}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(T\left[\smallint\Psi(\cdot,x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}\right](\mathbf{w}_{i})-(Th_{\circ})(\mathbf{w}_{i})\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x}^{\circ})-\mathbf{y}_{i}\Big)^{2}+\sigma_{2}\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x}^{\circ},\nu_{x})+(\dagger)+\frac{R^{2}(R+M)^{2}}{\sigma_{1}\lambda}+(R^{2}+RM)\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{n}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(T\left[\smallint\Psi(\cdot,x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}\right](\mathbf{w}_{i})-(Th_{\circ})(\mathbf{w}_{i})\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{Var}+M^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{n}}+\sigma_{2}\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x}^{\circ},\nu_{x})+(\dagger)+\frac{R^{2}(R+M)^{2}}{\sigma_{1}\lambda}+(R^{2}+RM)\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{n}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{P_{W}}\left[\Big(T\left[\smallint\Psi(\cdot,x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}\right](W)-(Th_{\circ})(W)\Big)^{2}\right]+(R+M)^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{n}}.

(75)

The last inequality holds by applying concentration inequalities.
In the last inequality above, notice that for each
i
, the error term

\mathbf{y}_{i}-\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x}^{\circ})=\mathbf{y}_{i}-(Th_{\circ})(\mathbf{w}_{i})
is zero-mean and bounded and hence
M
-subgaussian by
1
. If we denote
\mathrm{Var}\leq M^{2}
being the variance of
Y-(Th_{\circ})(W)
which corresponds to the Bayes optimal risk, then
from Corollary 2.8.3 in
78
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\smallint\Psi(\mathbf{w}_{i},z)\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x}^{\circ})-\mathbf{y}_{i}\Big)^{2}\leq\mathrm{Var}+M^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{n}}.

As proved in
9.7
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\nu_{x})\leq 2\sigma_{2}^{-1}R^{2}+M_{x}=:\mathfrak{M}_{x}.

(76)

Denote

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{B}}(\mathfrak{M}_{x}):=\left\{(\mathbf{w},\mathbf{y})\mapsto\big((Tf)(\mathbf{w})-\mathbf{y}\big)^{2}\big|\quad f:\mathcal{W}\to\mathbb{R}\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{M}_{x}}\right\}.

By the contraction lemma, we obtain that
\mathfrak{R}(\overline{\mathcal{B}}(\mathfrak{M}_{x})\leq(R+M)\cdot\mathfrak{R}(\mathcal{B}(\mathfrak{M}_{x}))
.
Utilizing the standard uniform bound
(
80
)
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{g\in\overline{\mathcal{B}}(\mathfrak{M}_{x})}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{P_{WY}}[g(W,Y)]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(\mathbf{w}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathfrak{R}(\mathfrak{M}_{x})+12R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{2n}}

\displaystyle\leq 2R(R+M)\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{M}_{x}}{n}}+12R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{2n}}.

The last inequality holds by using the upper bound on the empirical Rademacher complexity proved in
10.7
.
Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}_{P_{WY}}\left[\Big(T[\smallint\Psi(\cdot,x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}](W)-Y\Big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(T[\smallint\Psi(\cdot,x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}](\mathbf{w}_{i})-\mathbf{y}_{i}\Big)^{2}+2R(R+M)\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{M}_{x}}{n}}+12R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{2n}}.

Note that the first empirical mean squared error term can be upper bounded by
\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\mu_{z}^{\circ}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}))
which is upper bounded in Eq. (
75
) with with
P^{\otimes n}
probability at least
1-6\delta
.
We proceed from above to obtain,

\displaystyle\leq\eqref{eq:optimization_gap_2}+2R(R+M)\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{M}_{x}}{n}}+12R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{2n}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{Var}+M^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{n}}+\sigma_{2}\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x}^{\circ},\nu_{x})+(\dagger)+\frac{R^{2}(R+M)^{2}}{\sigma_{1}\lambda}+(R^{2}+RM)\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{n}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\Big(T\left[\smallint\Psi(\cdot,x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}\right](W)-(Th_{\circ})(W)\Big)^{2}\right]+(R+M)^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{n}}+R(R+M)\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{2}^{-1}R^{2}+M_{x}}{n}}.

Note that we have removed the unnecessary scalar factors to simplify the formula.
Next, we inspect the LHS of the above inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P_{WY}}\left[\Big(T[\smallint\Psi(\cdot,x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}](W)-Y\Big)^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\Big(T\left[\smallint\Psi(\cdot,x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}\right](W)-(Th_{\circ})(W)\Big)^{2}\right]+\mathrm{Var}.

Therefore, we reach, with
P^{\otimes n}
probability at least
1-6\delta
,

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}_{P_{W}}\left[\Big(T[\smallint\Psi(\cdot,x)\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}](W)-(Th_{\circ})(W)\Big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma_{2}M_{x}+(\dagger)+\frac{R^{2}(R+M)^{2}}{\sigma_{1}\lambda}+(R+M)^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{n}}+R(R+M)\sqrt{\frac{\frac{R^{2}}{\sigma_{2}}+M_{x}}{n}}.

Finally,
(\dagger)
denotes the generalization bound established in
9.6
. With probability at least
1-2\delta
under
P^{\otimes m}
, we have

(\dagger)\leq\sigma_{1}M_{z}+R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log\left(\delta^{-1}\right)}{m}}+R^{2}\sqrt{\frac{M_{z}+R^{2}/\sigma_{1}}{m}}
.
The proof is thus concluded.
∎

Lemma 9.7
.

Suppose
1
,
2
and
4
hold. Then, for any fixed
\lambda>0
, for
\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast}
defined in (

λ

-penalty
), we have
\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\nu_{x})\leq\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x}^{\circ},\nu_{x})+2\sigma_{2}^{-1}R^{2}
.

Proof.

By definition in (

λ

-penalty
), we have
(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast})=\arg\min_{\mu_{x},\mu_{z}}\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})+\lambda(\mathscr{F}_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})-\mathscr{F}_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})))
.
The proof of this lemma holds for both population and empirical distributions
\rho
in the definition of
\mathscr{F}_{1}
and
\mathscr{F}_{2}
, so we do not make this distinction in the following derivations.

\displaystyle\quad\sigma_{2}\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\nu_{x})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})^{2}]+\sigma_{2}\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\nu_{x})+\lambda(\mathscr{F}_{1}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast})-\mathscr{F}_{1}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast})))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\circ})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}^{\circ})^{2}]+\sigma_{2}\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{x}^{\circ},\nu_{x}).

The last inequality holds by the optimality of
(\mu_{x,\lambda}^{\ast},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast})
. The proof is thus concluded by the boundedness of
\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}
and
\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}
as per
2
.
∎

10
Proof of Lemmas and Auxiliary Results

10.1
Proof of
A.3

Proof.

From its optimality,
\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})
satisfies, for any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x})\right)\cdot\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]+\frac{\zeta_{1}}{2}\|z\|^{2}=0.

And similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x}))\right)\cdot\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]+\frac{\zeta_{1}}{2}\|z\|^{2}=0.

Subtract the above two equations,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=-\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\nu_{x}\right)\cdot\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]+\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})\right)\cdot\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)\cdot\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(-\epsilon\cdot\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\nu_{x}+\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)\cdot\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right].

Since the above equality holds for any
z
, and by the completeness assumption, we have
\epsilon\cdot\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\mathrm{d}\nu_{x}=\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})
for
(\mathbf{a},\mathbf{w})
-
\rho
almost everywhere. Therefore, we have concluded the proof for the first claim.

From its optimality,
\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})
and
\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})
satisfy, for any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\mathbf{y}\right)\cdot\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]+\lambda\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x})\right)\cdot\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]+\frac{\lambda\zeta_{1}}{2}\|z\|^{2}=0

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})-\mathbf{y}\right)\cdot\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]+\lambda\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x}))\right)\cdot\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt+\frac{\lambda\zeta_{1}}{2}\|z\|^{2}=0.

Subtract the above two equations

\displaystyle 0=-\lambda\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\nu_{x}\right)\cdot\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]+(\lambda+1)\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)\cdot\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]

Since the above equality holds for any
z
, and by the completeness assumption, we have
\frac{\lambda}{\lambda+1}\epsilon\cdot\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\mathrm{d}\nu_{x}=\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})
.
∎

Lemma 10.1

(Uniform boundedness of the second moment)
.

Let
U:\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}
be a functional that admits a well-defined Wasserstein gradient
\boldsymbol{\nabla}U(\mu):\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
for any
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Suppose
\boldsymbol{\nabla}U
satisfies
\|\boldsymbol{\nabla}U(\mu)(x)\|\leq R
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Consider the following particle update scheme with step size
\gamma\leq\frac{1}{\zeta_{2}}
and
\zeta_{2}>0
: for
i=1,\ldots,N_{x}
,

\displaystyle x_{t+1}^{(i)}=x_{t}^{(i)}-\gamma\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\nabla}U(\mu_{\mathscr{X}})(x_{t}^{(i)})+\zeta_{2}x_{t}^{(i)}\right)+\sqrt{2\gamma\sigma}\xi_{z,t}^{(i)}

Then, for any
t\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, we have a uniform upper bound on the second moment

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|x_{t}^{(i)}\|^{2}]\leq\mathbb{E}[\|x_{0}^{(i)}\|^{2}]+\frac{2}{\zeta_{2}}\left(\frac{R^{2}}{2\zeta_{2}}+\sigma d\right).

Proof.

The lemma is Lemma 1 in
60
and Lemma 1 in
73
.
∎

Proposition 10.2

(Lipschitzness of
U_{\lambda}
)
.

Suppose
2
holds.
Recall the definition of
U_{\lambda}:\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}})\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}})\to\mathbb{R}
in Eq. (
52
) that
U_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z},\mu_{z})=U_{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{z})+\lambda\cdot F_{1}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z})+\lambda\sigma_{1}\cdot\mathrm{Ent}(\tilde{\mu}_{z})-\lambda\cdot F_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})-\lambda\sigma_{1}\cdot\mathrm{Ent}(\mu_{z})
.
For any
\mu_{x},\mu_{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})
and any
\mu_{z}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}})
,

\displaystyle U_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}),\mu_{z})-U_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}),\mu_{z})\geq-\frac{R\lambda}{4\sigma_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mu_{x}-\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)^{2}\right]\geq-\frac{R^{3}\lambda}{4\sigma_{1}}\mathrm{TV}^{2}(\mu_{x},\mu_{x}^{\prime}).

Proof.

We start from the definition of
U_{\lambda}
:

\displaystyle\quad U_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}),\mu_{z})-U_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}),\mu_{z})

\displaystyle=U_{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))-U_{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}))+\lambda\cdot U_{1}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))-\lambda\cdot U_{1}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}))

(77)

\displaystyle\qquad+\lambda\frac{\zeta_{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})}\left[\|z\|^{2}\right]-\lambda\frac{\zeta_{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})}\left[\|z\|^{2}\right]+\lambda\sigma_{1}\cdot\mathrm{Ent}\left(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)-\lambda\sigma_{1}\cdot\mathrm{Ent}\left(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right).

(78)

Notice that

\displaystyle\quad\mathrm{Ent}\left(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)-\mathrm{Ent}\left(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle=\int\log(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z))\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z)\;\mathrm{d}z-\int\log(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})(z))\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})(z)\;\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\mathrm{KL}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}),\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}))+\int\log(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})(z))\Big(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z)-\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})(z)\Big)\;\mathrm{d}z.

Given the optimality of
\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})
, we know that the first variation of the mapping
\mu_{z}\mapsto\mathscr{F}_{2}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})+\lambda\mathscr{F}_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})
at
\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})
equals a constant
(
35
, Proposition 2.5)
.

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})-\mathbf{y}\right)\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]+\lambda\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})-\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}^{\prime}\right)\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\lambda\frac{\zeta_{2}}{2}\|z\|^{2}+\lambda\sigma_{1}\log\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})(z)=C^{\prime}.

Here,
C^{\prime}
is a constant that does not vary with
z
.
So we obtain

\displaystyle\quad\lambda\sigma_{1}\int\log(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})(z))\Big(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z)-\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})(z)\Big)\;\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\int\lambda\frac{\zeta_{2}}{2}\|z\|^{2}\;\left(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})-\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)+\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})-\mathbf{y}\right)\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\left(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})-\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\lambda\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})-\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}^{\prime}\right)\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\left(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})-\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)\right)\right].

So the term in Eq. (
78
) equals

(
78
)

\displaystyle=\lambda\sigma_{1}\mathrm{KL}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}),\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}))+\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})-\mathbf{y}\right)\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\left(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})-\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\lambda\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})-\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}^{\prime}\right)\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\left(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})-\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)\right)\right].

We also have, by definition of
U_{2}
,

\displaystyle U_{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))-U_{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})+\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-2\mathbf{y}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\left.\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\left(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)\right)\right].

And, by definition of
U_{1}
,

\displaystyle U_{1}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))-U_{1}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(-2\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}+\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})+\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\left.\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\left(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)\right)\right].

We plug the above two equations back to Eq. (
77
) which gives

(
77
)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(-2\lambda\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}+(1+\lambda)\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})+(1+\lambda)\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-2\mathbf{y}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\left(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)\right)\right].

Combine the above derivations about Eq. (
77
) and Eq. (
78
), we obtain

\displaystyle\quad U_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}),{\mu}_{z})-U_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}),{\mu}_{z})=\lambda\sigma_{1}\mathrm{KL}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}),\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(-\lambda\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}+\lambda\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}^{\prime}-\frac{1+\lambda}{2}\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})+\frac{1+\lambda}{2}\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\left(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{1+\lambda}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\left(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{R(1+\lambda)}{2}\mathrm{TV}^{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}),\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\geq-R(1+\lambda)\mathrm{KL}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}),\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})).

The first inequality holds by Eq. (
79
) in
10.4
and the last inequality holds by Pinsker’s inequality.
Finally, we apply Eq. (
80
) in
10.4
to conclude the proof.

\displaystyle U_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}),{\mu}_{z})-U_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}),{\mu}_{z})\geq-\frac{R\lambda}{4\sigma_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mu_{x}-\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)^{2}\right]\geq-\frac{R^{3}\lambda}{4\sigma_{1}}\mathrm{TV}^{2}(\mu_{x},\mu_{x}^{\prime}).

∎

Proposition 10.3

(Leave-one-out Lipschitzness of
\delta_{1}U_{\lambda}
and
\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}
)
.

Let
\mu_{\mathscr{X}}=\frac{1}{N_{x}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\delta_{x^{(i)}}
be the empirical distribution of
N_{x}
particles; and let
\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}}=\frac{1}{N_{x}}\sum_{j\neq i}\delta_{x^{(j)}}+\frac{1}{N_{x}}\delta_{x}
be another empirical distribution of
N_{x}
particles with the
i
-th particle replaced with
x
. Then we have, for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
,

\displaystyle\left|\delta_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}))(x)-\delta_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}}))(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N_{x}}\left(R^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\sigma_{1}}}R^{3}\right),

\displaystyle\left|\delta U_{2}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}))(x)-\delta U_{2}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}}))(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N_{x}}\left(R^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\sigma_{1}}}R^{3}\right).

And

\displaystyle\left|\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}))(x)-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}}))(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N_{x}}\left(R^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\sigma_{1}}}R^{3}\right),

\displaystyle\left|\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{2}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}))(x)-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{2}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}}))(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N_{x}}\left(R^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\sigma_{1}}}R^{3}\right).

Proof.

We have from
10.5
,

\displaystyle\quad\left|\delta_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}}))(x)-\delta_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}))(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq R\left|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\frac{1}{N_{x}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{x}}\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}(x^{(i)})-\left(\frac{1}{N_{x}}\sum_{j\neq i}\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}(x^{(j)})+\frac{1}{N_{x}}\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}(x)\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+R\left|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}})-\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{N_{x}}R^{2}+R^{2}\cdot\mathrm{TV}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}),\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}})).

The last inequality holds by the boundedness of
\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}
and the boundness of
\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}
.
Next, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}),\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}}))

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\mathrm{KL}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}),\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}}))}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{4\sigma_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}-\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}})\right)^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\sigma_{1}}}\frac{R}{N_{x}}.

The first inequality holds by Pinsker’s inequality, the second inequality holds by using
10.4
and the last inequality holds by the boundedness of
\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}
.
Therefore, we have shown that

\displaystyle\quad\left|\delta_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{\mathscr{X}}))(x)-\delta_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x\cup\mathscr{X}_{-i}}))(x)\right|\leq\frac{1}{N_{x}}\left(R^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\sigma_{1}}}R^{3}\right).

The similar derivations hold for the other three inequalities as well. So the proof is concluded.
∎

Proposition 10.4

(Continuity of the mappings
\mu_{x}\mapsto\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})
and
\mu_{x}\mapsto\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})
)
.

Suppose
2
holds.
Let
\mu_{x},\mu_{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})
. Let
\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})=\arg\min_{\mu_{z}}\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})=\arg\min_{\mu_{z}}U_{2}(\mu_{z})+\lambda F_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})+\lambda\sigma_{1}\mathrm{Ent}(\mu_{z})
in Eq. (
17
). Then, we have

\displaystyle\lambda\sigma_{1}\Big(\mathrm{KL}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}),\;\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}))+\mathrm{KL}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}),\;\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))\Big)

\displaystyle=-(1+\lambda)\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\left(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip-200.0003pt+\lambda\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\left(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)\right)\cdot\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mu_{x}-\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)\right]

(79)

\displaystyle\lambda\sigma_{1}\Big(\mathrm{KL}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}),\;\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}))+\mathrm{KL}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}),\;\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda^{2}}{4(1+\lambda)}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mu_{x}-\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda^{2}R^{2}}{4(1+\lambda)}\mathrm{TV}^{2}(\mu_{x},\mu_{x}^{\prime}).

(80)

Similarly, let
\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})=\arg\min_{\mu_{z}}\mathscr{F}_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})=\arg\min_{\mu_{z}}F_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})+\sigma_{1}\mathrm{Ent}(\mu_{z})
in Eq. (
16
). Then,

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}\Big(\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}),\;\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}))+\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}),\;\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mu_{x}-\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{R^{2}}{4}\mathrm{TV}^{2}(\mu_{x},\mu_{x}^{\prime}).

(81)

Proof.

From the optimality of
\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})
, the first variation of the mapping
\mu_{z}\mapsto U_{2}(\mu_{z})+\lambda F_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})+\lambda\sigma_{1}\mathrm{Ent}(\mu_{z})

(
35
, Proposition 2.5)
equals a constant, for any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}}
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\mathbf{y}\right)\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]+\lambda\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}\right)\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\lambda\frac{\zeta_{2}}{2}\|z\|^{2}+\lambda\sigma_{1}\log\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z)=C,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})-\mathbf{y}\right)\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]+\lambda\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})-\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}^{\prime}\right)\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\lambda\frac{\zeta_{2}}{2}\|z\|^{2}+\lambda\sigma_{1}\log\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})(z)=C^{\prime},

where
C,C^{\prime}
are two constants that do not vary with
z
.
If we subtract the above two equations, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int(1+\lambda)\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\left(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)-\lambda\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mu_{x}-\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\lambda\sigma_{1}\left(\log\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z)-\log\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})(z)\right)=C-C^{\prime}.

(82)

Take expectation with respect to
\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})
for both sides of Eq. (
82
), then we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int(1+\lambda)\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\left(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)-\lambda\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mu_{x}-\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)\cdot\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\lambda\sigma_{1}\mathrm{KL}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}),\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))=C-C^{\prime}.

Take expectation of Eq. (
82
) with respect to
\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})
on both sides, then we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int(1+\lambda)\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\left(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)-\lambda\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mu_{x}-\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)\cdot\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\lambda\sigma_{1}\mathrm{KL}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}),\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}))=C-C^{\prime}.

We subtract the above two equations, and obtain

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int(1+\lambda)\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\left(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)-\lambda\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mu_{x}-\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)\cdot\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\lambda\sigma_{1}\mathrm{KL}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}),\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))+\lambda\sigma_{1}\mathrm{KL}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}),\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}))=0.

After a reordering of the above equality, we have

\displaystyle\quad\lambda\sigma_{1}\mathrm{KL}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}),\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))+\lambda\sigma_{1}\mathrm{KL}(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}),\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=-(1+\lambda)\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\left(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\lambda\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\left(\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)\right)\cdot\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mu_{x}-\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)\right].

So we have finished the proof for Eq. (
79
).
Notice that the right hand side of Eq. (
79
) can be further upper bounded by,

\displaystyle\text{RHS of }\eqref{eq:continuity_one}\leq\frac{\lambda^{2}}{4(1+\lambda)}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mu_{x}-\mu_{x}^{\prime})\right)^{2}\right]\leq\frac{\lambda^{2}R^{2}}{4(1+\lambda)}\mathrm{TV}^{2}(\mu_{x},\mu_{x}^{\prime}),

where we use the fact
ab\leq\frac{1+\lambda}{\lambda}a^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{4(1+\lambda)}b^{2}
for the first inequality and the boundness of
\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}
for the second inequality.
So we have finished the proof for Eq. (
80
).
The same derivations hold for the proof of Eq. (
81
) as well.
∎

Lemma 10.5

(Lipschitz continuity of Wasserstein gradient and first variation of
U_{1},U_{2}
)
.

Suppose
2
and
1
hold.
Let
U_{1},U_{2}
be defined in (
Bi-MFLD
).
For any
i,j\in\{1,2\}
, we have the following Lipschitz continuity of the Wasserstein gradients: for any
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
, any
\mu_{x,1},\mu_{x,2}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})
and any
\mu_{z,1},\mu_{z,2}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}})

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\quad\left\|\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{i}U_{j}(\mu_{x,1},\mu_{z,1})(x_{1})-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{i}U_{j}(\mu_{x,2},\mu_{z,2})(x_{2})\right\|\\
&\leq R\left|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;(\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,1}-\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,2})\right]\right|+R\left|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;(\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,1}-\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,2})\right]\right|+R^{2}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|\\
&\leq R^{2}\left(W_{2}(\mu_{x,1},\mu_{x,2})+W_{2}(\mu_{z,1},\mu_{z,2})+\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|\right).\end{aligned}

(83)

For any
i,j\in\{1,2\}
, we have the following Lipschitz continuity of the first variations: for any
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
, any
\mu_{x,1},\mu_{x,2}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})
and any
\mu_{z,1},\mu_{z,2}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}})

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\quad\left|\delta_{i}U_{j}(\mu_{x,1},\mu_{z,1})(x_{1})-\delta_{i}U_{j}(\mu_{x,2},\mu_{z,2})(x_{2})\right|\\
&\leq R\left|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;(\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,1}-\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,2})\right]\right|+R\left|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;(\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,1}-\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,2})\right]\right|+R^{2}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|\\
&\leq R^{2}\left(W_{2}(\mu_{x,1},\mu_{x,2})+W_{2}(\mu_{z,1},\mu_{z,2})+\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|\right).\end{aligned}

(84)

We have the following Lipschitz continuity of the objectives themselves: for any
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
, any
\mu_{x,1},\mu_{x,2}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})
and any
\mu_{z,1},\mu_{z,2}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}})

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\quad\left|U_{1}(\mu_{x,1},\mu_{z,1})-U_{1}(\mu_{x,2},\mu_{z,2})\right|\\
&\leq 2R\left|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;(\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,1}-\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,2})\right]\right|+2R\left|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;(\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,1}-\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,2})\right]\right|\\
&\leq 2R^{2}\left(W_{2}(\mu_{x,1},\mu_{x,2})+W_{2}(\mu_{z,1},\mu_{z,2})\right).\end{aligned}

(85)

And

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left|U_{2}(\mu_{z,1})-U_{2}(\mu_{z,2})\right|\leq(R+M)\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left|\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,1}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,2}\right|\right]\leq R(R+M)W_{2}(\mu_{z,1},\mu_{z,2}).\end{aligned}

(86)

Proof.

From the definition of
U_{1},U_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\left|\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{x,1},\mu_{z,1})(x_{1})-\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{x,2},\mu_{z,2})(x_{2})\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,1}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,1}\right)\cdot\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}(x_{1})\right]\right.-\left.\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,2}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,2}\right)\cdot\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}(x_{2})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq R\left|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;(\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,1}-\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,2})\right]\right|+R\left|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;(\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,1}-\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,2})\right]\right|+R^{2}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|

\displaystyle\leq R^{2}\left(W_{2}(\mu_{x,1},\mu_{x,2})+W_{2}(\mu_{z,1},\mu_{z,2})+\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|\right).

The same proof applies to the Lipschitzness of
\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{2}U_{1}
,
\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{1}U_{2}
and
\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{2}U_{2}
as well.
So we have finished the proof of Eq. (
83
).
Next, following the same derivations, we have

\displaystyle\quad\left|\delta_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{x,1},\mu_{z,1})(x_{1})-\delta_{1}U_{1}(\mu_{x,2},\mu_{z,2})(x_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\leq R\left|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;(\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,1}-\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,2})\right]\right|+R\left|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;(\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,1}-\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,2})\right]\right|+R^{2}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|

\displaystyle\leq R^{2}\left(W_{2}(\mu_{x,1},\mu_{x,2})+W_{2}(\mu_{z,1},\mu_{z,2})+\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|\right).

So we have finished the proof of Eq. (
84
).
Finally, we have

\displaystyle\quad\left|U_{1}(\mu_{x,1},\mu_{z,1})-U_{1}(\mu_{x,2},\mu_{z,2})\right|=\frac{1}{2}\left|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,1}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,1}\right)^{2}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\qquad-\left.\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x,2}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,2}\right)^{2}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2R\cdot\left|\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mu_{x,1}-\mu_{x,2})\right|+2R\left|\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mu_{z,1}-\mu_{z,2})\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2R^{2}W_{2}(\mu_{x,1},\mu_{x,2})+2R^{2}W_{2}(\mu_{z,1},\mu_{z,2}).

And

\displaystyle\left|U_{2}(\mu_{z,1})-U_{2}(\mu_{z,2})\right|=\frac{1}{2}\left|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,1}-\mathbf{y}\right)^{2}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,2}-\mathbf{y}\right)^{2}\right]\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,1}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,2}\right)\cdot\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,1}+\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,2}-2\mathbf{y}\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq(R+M)\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left|\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,1}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z,2}\right|\right]\leq R(R+M)W_{2}(\mu_{z,1},\mu_{z,2}).

So we have finished the proof.
∎

Lemma 10.6
.

Let
V:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be an admissible transport map. Let
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with a
C^{1}
density function
p:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
. Define
\phi_{t}:=\mathrm{Id}+tV
for
0<t<1
. Denote
q
as the density of another probability measure
(\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu={\phi_{t}}_{\#}\mu
. Then, we have
\nabla\log q(\phi_{t}(x))=\nabla\log p(x)-t\cdot\mathbf{J}V(x)\nabla\log p(x)-t\cdot\nabla\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{J}V(x))+o(t)
and
\nabla\log p(\phi_{t}(x))=\nabla\log p(x)+t\cdot\mathbf{H}\log p(x)V(x)+o(t)
.

Proof.

From the change of variables formula, we have
q(\phi_{t}(x))=\frac{p(x)}{|\mathrm{Id}+t\mathbf{J}V(x)|}
. Hence, we have
q(x)=\frac{p(\phi_{t}^{-1}(x))}{|\mathrm{Id}+t\mathbf{J}V(\phi_{t}^{-1}(x))|}
.

\displaystyle\nabla\log q(x)=\frac{\nabla p(\phi_{t}^{-1}(x))^{\top}\left[\mathbf{J}\phi_{t}(\phi_{t}^{-1}(x))\right]^{-1}}{p(\phi_{t}^{-1}(x))}-t\cdot\left[\mathbf{J}\phi_{t}(\phi_{t}^{-1}(x))\right]^{-1}F,

where
F=[F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
F_{i}=\mathrm{Tr}([\mathrm{Id}+t\mathbf{J}V(\phi_{t}^{-1}(x))]^{-1}\nabla_{x_{i}}\mathbf{J}V(\phi_{t}^{-1}(x)))
.
Hence,

\displaystyle\nabla\log q(\phi_{t}(x))=\frac{\nabla p(x)^{\top}\left[\mathbf{J}\phi_{t}(x)\right]^{-1}}{p(x)}-t\cdot\left[\mathbf{J}\phi_{t}(x)\right]^{-1}\tilde{F},

where
\tilde{F}=[\tilde{F}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{F}_{d}]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\tilde{F}_{i}=\mathrm{Tr}([\mathrm{Id}+t\mathbf{J}V(x)]^{-1}\nabla_{x_{i}}\mathbf{J}V(x))
.
Since
V:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is an admissible transport map, its derivatives are infinitely times differentiable over a compact domain, so we have
\left[\mathbf{J}\phi_{t}(x)\right]^{-1}=[\mathrm{Id}+t\mathbf{J}V(x)]^{-1}=\mathrm{Id}-t\mathbf{J}V(x)+o(t)
.
So we obtain
