\displaystyle\nabla\log q(\phi_{t}(x))=\nabla\log p(x)-t\cdot\mathbf{J}V(x)\nabla\log p(x)-t\cdot\nabla\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{J}V(x))+o(t).

Repeating the same steps as above, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla\log p(\phi_{t}(x))=\nabla\log p(x)+t\cdot\mathbf{H}\log p(x)V(x)+o(t).

∎

Lemma 10.7

(Rademacher complexity)
.

Suppose
2
holds.
Consider a KL restricted Barron space:

\mathcal{B}_{M}:=\{\smallint\Psi(\cdot,x)\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\mid\mathrm{KL}(\mu,\nu)\leq M\}
, where
\nu=\mathcal{N}(0,\zeta_{1}\sigma_{1}^{-1}\mathrm{Id}_{d})
. Then, the Rademacher complexity of
\mathcal{B}_{M}
satisfies
\mathfrak{R}(\mathcal{B}_{M})=\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{n^{-1}R^{2}M})
.

Proof.

Since Rademacher complexity is smaller than Gaussian complexity, it suffices to bound the Gaussian complexity
\mathfrak{G}(\mathcal{B}_{M}):=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)}[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{B}_{M}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}f(\mathbf{a}_{i})]
. Let
Z(x):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\Psi(\mathbf{a}_{i},x)
. Note that
Z(x)
follows a Gaussian distribution with mean 0 and variance
\sigma(x)^{2}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Psi(\mathbf{a}_{i},x)^{2}\leq R^{2}
as per
2
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}(\mathcal{B}_{M})

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{B}_{M}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}f(\mathbf{a}_{i})\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\sup_{\mathrm{KL}(\mu,\nu)\leq M}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\int\Psi(\mathbf{a}_{i},x)\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\sup_{\mathrm{KL}(\mu,\nu)\leq M}\int Z(x)\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\right]\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\sup_{\mathrm{KL}(\mu,\nu)\leq M}\sqrt{\int Z(x)^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\sup_{\mathrm{KL}(\mu,\nu)\leq M}\int Z(x)^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\right]}.

From the Donsker-Varadhan duality formula of the KL-divergence, we have, for any
\gamma>0
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\gamma}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\sup_{\mathrm{KL}(\mu,\nu)\leq M}\gamma\int Z(x)^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\gamma}\left\{M+\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\log\int\exp\left(\gamma Z(x)^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}\nu(x)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\gamma}\left\{M+\log\int\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\exp\left(\gamma Z(x)^{2}\right)\right]\mathrm{d}\nu(x)\right\}.

Since
Z(x)
is a zero mean Gaussian random variable with variance
\sigma(x)\leq R^{2}
. We have, taking
\gamma=1/(4R^{2})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\exp\left(\gamma Z(x)^{2}\right)\right]=\sqrt{\frac{1}{1-2\gamma\sigma(x)^{2}}}\leq\sqrt{2}.

We have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}\left(\mathcal{B}_{M}\right)\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{4R^{2}(M+\log\sqrt{2})}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{R^{2}M}{n}}.

The proof is concluded.
∎

Appendix A
Additional theoretical results

A.1
Convexity of
\mu_{x}\mapsto L_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))
.

Proposition A.1

(Convexity of
\mu_{x}\mapsto L_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))
)
.

The mapping
\mu_{x}\mapsto L_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))
is
linear
convex under the following additional conditions.

1)
\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})
and
\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})
coincide with the global optima of their respective objectives excluding entropic regularization in the space of signed measures. In other words,
\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})=\arg\min_{\mu_{z}\in\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}})}F_{2}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})+\lambda F_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})
and
\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})=\arg\min_{\mu_{z}\in\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}})}F_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})
.

2) For any bounded measurable function
f\in L^{\infty}(\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{W})
, if there is
\mathbb{E}_{(\mathbf{a},\mathbf{w})\sim\rho}[f(\mathbf{a},\mathbf{w})\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)]=0
for any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}}
, then
f(\mathbf{a},\mathbf{w})=0
holds
\rho
-almost surely.

The proof can be found later in this section.
The convexity of
\mu_{z}\mapsto L_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})
for fixed
\mu_{x}
is straightforward given the partial convexity proved in
4.1
.
In contrast, analyzing the convexity of the mapping
\mu_{x}\mapsto L_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))
is more subtle due to the nested structure and the subtraction in the definition of
L_{\lambda}
in Eq. (
15
). Fortunately,
L_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))
can be written— ignoring the entropic regularizations as well as the terms in
\mathscr{F}_{1}
that are constant in
\mu_{x}
— as

\displaystyle L_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))=\min_{\mu_{z}}\Big(F_{2}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})+\lambda F_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})\Big)-\min_{\mu_{z}}\Big(\lambda F_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})\Big)+C,

which expresses
L_{\lambda}
as the difference between the minima of two quadratic functionals.
Since the first functional is “more quadratic” than the second, it is still possible to establish convexity of the overall expression by carefully examining the second-order derivative—despite the subtraction.
However, this convexity result holds only when we ignore the manifold structure of the space of probability measures, i.e., when working over the space of signed measures
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
instead of the probability simplex
\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
As a result, the entropic regularization are not well-defined over signed measures and also need to be ignored.
See
A.3
for details.

Remark A.2

(Restrictiveness of the conditions of
A.1
)
.

The first condition in
A.1
requires access to the global optimum in the space of signed measures, which is a challenging task even in the mean-field limit of neural networks. To the best of our knowledge, the only works addressing this problem are
75
and
82
, both of which require the second-layer weights to be trained at a much faster rate than those of the first layer. Moreover, these works consider unbounded neural networks, which are not compatible with our
2
.

The second completeness condition in
A.1
is equivalent to the condition that the
\mathrm{span}\{(\mathbf{a},\mathbf{w})\mapsto\rho(\mathbf{a},\mathbf{w})\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\mid z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}}\}
is dense in
L^{1}(\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{W})
, a consequence of the duality between
L^{1}(\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{W})
and
L^{\infty}(\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{W})
.
This condition excludes, in particular, the case where
\rho
is an empirical distribution.
Owing to the restrictiveness of both conditions in
A.1
, we do not assume convexity of the mapping
\mu_{x}\mapsto L_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))
in our paper. The absence of convexity introduces substantial difficulties in the convergence analysis in
Section

5
.

Proof of
A.1
.

The proof relies on the following
A.3
proved in
Section

10.1
. It uses the formal definition of first-order and second-order Fréchet derivatives on the space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, treated as a Banach space equipped with the total variation norm
[
62
, Chapter 3]
. Specifically, these derivatives are expressed via dual representations, leveraging the fact that the dual space of
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is
C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the space of bounded continuous functions
[
8
, Chapter 8]
.

Lemma A.3
.

Fix any
\mu_{x}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})
.
Let
\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})=\arg\min_{\mu_{z}\in\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}})}F_{2}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})+\lambda F_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})
and
\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})=\arg\min_{\mu_{z}\in\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}})}F_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})
.
Suppose for any bounded measurable function
f:\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{W}\to\mathbb{R}
, if there is
\mathbb{E}_{(\mathbf{a},\mathbf{w})\sim\rho}[f(\mathbf{a},\mathbf{w})\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)]=0
for any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}}
, then
f(\mathbf{a},\mathbf{w})=0
holds
\rho
almost surely.
Then, for any
\nu_{x}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})
and
\epsilon>0
, the following two equations hold
\rho
almost everywhere:

\displaystyle\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})

\displaystyle=\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})+\epsilon\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\nu_{x}

\displaystyle\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})

\displaystyle=\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})+\frac{\lambda}{\lambda+1}\epsilon\int\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\nu_{x}.

Definition 6

(Dual representations of first-order and second-order Fréchet derivatives)
.

For a functional
\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}
, its
first-order Fréchet derivative
at
\mu
denoted as
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
satisfies
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\epsilon}|_{\epsilon=0}\mathcal{F}(\mu+\epsilon\nu)=\int\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}\mathrm{d}\nu
for any direction
\nu
.
Its
second-order Fréchet derivative
(also referred to as the
Fréchet Hessian
) at
\mu
, denoted by
\mathbf{H}_{\mu}\mathcal{F}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
satisfies
\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}\epsilon^{2}}|_{\epsilon=0}\mathcal{F}(\mu+\epsilon\nu)=\int\mathbf{H}_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(x,x^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\nu(x)\mathrm{d}\nu(x^{\prime})
for any direction
\nu
.

Next, we consider the Fréchet Hessian of the following two maps:

\displaystyle\mu_{x}\mapsto\lambda F_{1}\left(\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{*}(\mu_{x})\right):=L_{1}(\mu_{x}),\quad\mu_{x}\mapsto F_{2}\left(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{*}(\mu_{x})\right)+\lambda F_{1}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{*}(\mu_{x})):=L_{2}(\mu_{x}).

(A.1)

The proof is divided into two parts, the first part proves that
\mathbf{H}_{\mu_{x}}L_{1}(\mu_{x})=0
and the second part proves that
\mathbf{H}_{\mu_{x}}L_{2}(\mu_{x})=\frac{\lambda}{2(\lambda+1)}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\otimes\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}]
.
From
A.3
, for a fixed
\nu_{x}
, consider

\displaystyle\quad L_{1}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x}))

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\mathrm{d}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})\right)^{2}\right]+\frac{\lambda\zeta_{1}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})}[\|z\|^{2}]

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})+\epsilon\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\nu_{x}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\mathrm{d}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})\right)^{2}\right]+\frac{\lambda\zeta_{1}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})}[\|z\|^{2}]

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}\right)^{2}\right]+\frac{\lambda\zeta_{1}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})}[\|z\|^{2}]

\displaystyle=L_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))-\frac{\lambda\zeta_{1}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})}[\|z\|^{2}]+\frac{\lambda\zeta_{1}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})}[\|z\|^{2}].

So,

\displaystyle\quad L_{1}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x}))-L_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))=-\frac{\lambda\zeta_{1}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})}[\|z\|^{2}]+\frac{\lambda\zeta_{1}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})}[\|z\|^{2}]

\displaystyle=\lambda\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x})\right)\cdot\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})\right)\right]\Big)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\lambda\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x})\right)\cdot\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\nu_{x}\right]\Big).

The second last equality holds by the optimality of
\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})
and
\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})
; the last equality holds by using
A.3
.
A quick sanity check of the above derivations is to notice that the first-order Fréchet derivative
\delta_{\mu_{x}}L_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))=-\lambda\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}\right)\cdot\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot)\right]
, which agrees with the derivation from the envelope theorem using the optimality of
\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}\epsilon^{2}}\mid_{\epsilon=0}L_{1}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x}))=0,\quad\Rightarrow\quad\mathbf{H}_{\mu_{x}}L_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))=0.

Next, we are about to compute and show that
\mathbf{H}_{\mu_{x}}L_{2}(\mu_{x})=\frac{\lambda}{2(\lambda+1)}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\otimes\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}]
.
From
A.3
, consider

\displaystyle\quad L_{2}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})-\mathbf{y}\right)^{2}\right]+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x}}[\|x\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt+\frac{\lambda}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\mathrm{d}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})\right)^{2}\right]+\frac{\lambda\zeta_{1}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})}[\|z\|^{2}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})+\frac{\lambda}{\lambda+1}\epsilon\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\nu_{x}-\mathbf{y}\right)^{2}\right]+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x}}[\|x\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{\lambda}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})+\frac{\lambda}{\lambda+1}\epsilon\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\nu_{x}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\mathrm{d}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})\right)^{2}\right]+\frac{\lambda\zeta_{1}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})}[\|z\|^{2}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\mathbf{y}\right)^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\frac{\lambda}{\lambda+1}\epsilon\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\nu_{x}\right)\cdot\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\mathbf{y}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\lambda}{\lambda+1}\epsilon\right)^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\nu_{x}\right)^{2}]+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x}}[\|x\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\lambda}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}\right)^{2}\right]-\lambda\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\frac{1}{\lambda+1}\epsilon\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\nu_{x}\right)\cdot\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{\lambda}{2}\left(\frac{1}{\lambda+1}\epsilon\right)^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\nu_{x}\right)^{2}]+\frac{\lambda\zeta_{1}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})}[\|z\|^{2}]

\displaystyle=L_{2}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))-\frac{\lambda\zeta_{1}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})}[\|z\|^{2}]+\frac{\lambda\zeta_{1}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})}[\|z\|^{2}]+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{2}\epsilon^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{x}}[\|x\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{\lambda}{2(\lambda+1)}\epsilon^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\nu_{x}\right)^{2}]+\frac{\lambda}{\lambda+1}\epsilon\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\nu_{x}\right)\cdot\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}-\mathbf{y}\right)\right].

Also, from the optimality condition of
\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})
and
\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})
, we have

\displaystyle-\frac{\lambda\zeta_{1}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})}[\|z\|^{2}]+\frac{\lambda\zeta_{1}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})}[\|z\|^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})-\mathbf{y}\right)\cdot(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x}))\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x}))\right)\cdot(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x}))\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\mathbf{y}\right)\cdot(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))\right]-\lambda\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x})\right)\cdot(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda}{\lambda+1}\epsilon\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x})\cdot(2\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\mathbf{y})\right]+\left(\frac{\lambda}{\lambda+1}\epsilon\right)^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda\cdot\left(-\frac{1}{1+\lambda}\epsilon(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x})\cdot(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))+\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda}\epsilon(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x})\cdot(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\lambda\cdot\frac{\lambda}{(1+\lambda)^{2}}\epsilon^{2}(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda}{\lambda+1}\epsilon\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\nu_{x})\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\mathbf{y}+\lambda\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\lambda\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x})\right)\right].

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\quad L_{2}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x}))-L_{2}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda}{2(\lambda+1)}\epsilon^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\nu_{x}\right)^{2}]+\lambda\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\nu_{x})\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x})\right)\right]+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{2}\epsilon^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{x}}[\|x\|^{2}].

A quick sanity check of the above derivations is to know that the first variation
\delta_{\mu_{x}}L_{2}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))(x)=\lambda\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}(x)\cdot\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x})\right)\right]
, which agrees with the derivation from the envelope theorem using the optimality of
\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\quad\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}\epsilon^{2}}\mid_{\epsilon=0}L_{2}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}+\epsilon\nu_{x}))=\frac{\lambda}{\lambda+1}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\nu_{x}\right)^{2}]+\zeta_{2}\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{x}}[\|x\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad\mathbf{H}_{\mu_{x}}L_{2}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))=\frac{\lambda}{\lambda+1}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\otimes\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}]+\zeta_{2}\mathrm{Id}.

which is a positive definite operator. Finally, from the definition of
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
in Eq. (
A.1
), and that
L_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))=L_{1}(\mu_{x})+L_{2}(\mu_{x})
, we can conclude that the Frechet Hessian of the mapping
\mu_{x}\mapsto L_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))
is positive definite. Hence, the mapping
\mu_{x}\mapsto L_{\lambda}(\mu_{x},\tilde{\mu}_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))
is convex.
∎

A.2
Implicit gradient method

In this section, we are about to show that solving (
Bi-MFLD
) without resorting to its Lagrangian formulation would lead to a Wasserstein gradient that is hard to approximate with finite particles.
This approach is known as implicit gradient method in the literature of bilevel optimization
[
27
]
.
In particular, unlike variational integrals defined in Equation 10.4.1 of
1
, the mapping of the outer level objective
\mu_{x}\mapsto F_{2}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))
does not preserve a structure where the Wasserstein gradient can be expressed simply as the gradient of its first variation. Consequently, we must revert to the original definition of the Fréchet subdifferential, as introduced in Section 10 of
1
.

In the following proposition,
\mathbf{J}f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
denotes the Jacobian of a mapping
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbf{H}f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
denotes the Hessian of a mapping
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
,
\otimes
denotes the tensor product.

Proposition A.4
.

Suppose
1
and
2
hold.
Suppose that
\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W},z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}}}\|\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq(4R)^{-1}\zeta_{1}
and that
\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(\cdot)\otimes\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(\cdot)]\succeq\Lambda\mathrm{Id}\succ 0
.
For any
\mu_{z}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}})
that admits density, define an operator
\mathfrak{G}_{\mu_{z}}:L^{2}(\mu_{z})\to L^{2}(\mu_{z})
as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{\mu_{z}}[V]=\left(z\mapsto-\mathbf{J}V(z)\nabla\log\mu_{z}(z)-\nabla\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{J}V(z))-\mathbf{H}\log\mu_{z}(z)V(z)\right).

Then, the mapping
\mu_{x}\mapsto F_{2}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))
admits the following Fréchet subdifferential

\displaystyle\xi:x\mapsto\zeta_{2}x+\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}^{\ast}g_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})}(x),

(A.2)

where the above
g_{\mu_{z}}(\cdot):=\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[(\int\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}-\mathbf{y})\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(\cdot)]\in L^{2}(\mu_{z})
and

\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}:=\big(\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(\cdot)\otimes\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(\cdot)]+\sigma_{1}\mathfrak{G}_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})}\big)^{-1}(\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(\cdot)\otimes\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot)])
is a bounded linear operator from
L^{2}(\mu_{x})
to
L^{2}(\mu_{z})
.

The proof can be found in
Section

A.2.1
.
The derivative conditions imposed on the network
\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}
are technical assumptions ensuring the existence of a Fréchet subdifferential, which intuitively, guarantee the invertibility condition underlying the implicit function theorem
[
40
]
.
However, since we are working in
\mathcal{P}_{2}
, a direct application of the implicit function theorem is not warranted (see
44
).
Instead, we adopt a constructive approach: we explicitly build the candidate differential maps and then verify that they indeed coincide with the Fréchet subdifferential (see
A.5
).

A.2.1
Proof of
A.4

Proof of
A.4
.

At the start of the proof, we are about to verify that
\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(\cdot)\otimes\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(\cdot)]+\sigma_{1}\mathfrak{G}_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})}
is a strictly positive definite operator such that its inverse is well-defined.
For any
V\in L^{2}(\mu_{z})
, we have
\langle V,\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(\cdot)\otimes\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(\cdot)]V\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu_{z})}=\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\langle V,\;\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu_{z})}^{2}]>0
.
Next, we consider
\langle V,\;\mathfrak{G}_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})}[V]\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))}
, which consists of two terms.
The first term is,

\displaystyle\quad\int\left(-V(z)^{\top}\mathbf{J}V(z)\nabla\log\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z)-V(z)^{\top}\nabla\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{J}V(z))\right)\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=-\int V(z)^{\top}\mathbf{J}V(z)\nabla\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z)\;\mathrm{d}z-\int V(z)^{\top}\nabla\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{J}V(z))\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z)\;\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\int\nabla\cdot\left(V(z)^{\top}\mathbf{J}V(z)\right)\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z)\;\mathrm{d}z-\int V(z)^{\top}\nabla\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{J}V(z))\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z)\;\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\int\|\mathbf{J}V(z)\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z)\geq 0,

where the last two equalities hold by integration by parts.
The second term is,

\displaystyle\quad-\int V(z)^{\top}\sigma_{1}\mathbf{H}\log\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z)V(z)\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z)

\displaystyle=\int V(z)^{\top}\Big(\zeta_{1}+\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}\right)\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]\Big)V(z)\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z)

\displaystyle\geq\|V\|_{L^{2}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))}^{2}\Big(\zeta_{1}-\|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}\right)\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]\|_{\mathrm{op}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\|V\|_{L^{2}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))}^{2}\Big(\zeta_{1}-|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}\right]|\cdot\sup_{\mathbf{w},z}\|\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\|V\|_{L^{2}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))}^{2}(\zeta_{1}-2R\cdot\sup_{\mathbf{w},z}\|\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\|_{\mathrm{op}})>\frac{\zeta_{1}}{2}\|V\|_{L^{2}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))}^{2},

provided that
\zeta_{1}\geq 4R\sup_{\mathbf{w},z}\|\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
as in the statement of the proposition.
Therefore, we have verified that

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}\left\langle V,\mathfrak{G}_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})}[V]\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))}\geq\frac{\zeta_{1}}{2}\|V\|_{L^{2}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))}^{2}.

So
\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(\cdot)\otimes\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(\cdot)]+\sigma_{1}\mathfrak{G}_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})}
is indeed a positive definite operator
\succeq\frac{\zeta_{1}}{2}\mathrm{Id}
so that its inverse is well-defined.
Furthermore, we have proved that its inverse is bounded hence
\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}:L^{2}(\mu_{x})\to L^{2}(\mu_{z})
is a bounded linear operator whose adjoint
\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}^{\ast}:L^{2}(\mu_{z})\to L^{2}(\mu_{x})
is well-defined.

To prove that
\xi
defined in Eq. (
A.2
) is the Fréchet subdifferential of
\mu_{x}\mapsto F_{2}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))
, we use
1
, which we recall below.

Definition 7

(Fréchet subdifferential
[
1
]
)
.

Let
\phi:\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}
be a proper and lower semicontinuous functional.
We say that
\xi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}\in L^{2}(\mu)
belongs to the Fréchet subdifferential of
\phi
if for any
\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, with
T_{\mu}^{\nu}
being the optimal transport map from
\mu
to
\nu
,

\displaystyle\phi(\nu)-\phi(\mu)\geq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\xi(x)^{\top}(T_{\mu}^{\nu}(x)-x)\;\mathrm{d}\mu(x)+o(W_{2}(\mu,\nu)).

Let
\nu_{x}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})
and
V:\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
be the optimal transport map from
\mu_{x}
to
\nu_{x}
.
Consider

\displaystyle\quad F_{2}((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x}))-F_{2}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\mathbf{y}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x})\right)^{2}\right]+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{(\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x}}[\|x\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\mathbf{y}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)^{2}\right]-\frac{\zeta_{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{x}}[\|x\|^{2}]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\mathbf{y}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x})\right)^{2}-\left(\mathbf{y}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)^{2}\right]}_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}

(A.3)

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\mathbf{y}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)^{2}\right]-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\mathbf{y}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)^{2}\right]}_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\zeta_{2}t\int x^{\top}V(x)\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}+o(t).

For the first term
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, from
A.5
, we have proved that
W_{2}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x}),(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))=o(t)
. Since
\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(\cdot)
is Lipschitz as per
2
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta:=\left|\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right|=o(t).

So we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\big(2\mathbf{y}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\big)\cdot\Delta\right]

which is still
o(t)
because
|\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)|
is uniformly bounded for any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}}
and
\mathbf{y}
is also uniformly bounded by
1
.

Now, for the other term
\mathcal{E}_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\mathbf{y}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z))\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)^{2}\right]-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\mathbf{y}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\mathbf{y}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-t\cdot\smallint\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)^{\top}\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z)\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})+o(t)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\mathbf{y}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=t\cdot\Big\langle\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\mathbf{y}\right)\cdot\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\right],\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V]\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))}+o(t)

\displaystyle=t\cdot\left\langle g_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})},\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V]\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))}+o(t).

The last equality holds by the definition of
g_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})}
in the statement of the proposition.
We now combine the above two terms and plug them back to Eq. (
A.2.1
) and we can thus obtain

\displaystyle\quad F_{2}((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x}))-F_{2}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))

\displaystyle=\zeta_{2}t\int x^{\top}V(x)\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}+t\cdot\left\langle g_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})},\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V]\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))}+o(t)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\zeta_{2}\mathrm{Id}+\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}^{\ast}g_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})},\;t\cdot V\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu_{x})}+o(t).

Finally, we apply the definition of Fréchet subdifferential in
7
and prove that
x\mapsto\zeta_{2}x+\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}^{\ast}[g_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})}](x)
is indeed the Fréchet subdifferential of the mapping
\mu_{x}\mapsto F_{2}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))
.
∎

Proposition A.5
.

Suppose
1
and
2
hold.
Recall
\mathfrak{G}_{\mu_{z}}:L^{2}(\mu_{z})\to L^{2}(\mu_{z})
defined in
A.4
.
Let
\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}
be a mapping from
L^{2}(\mu_{x})
to
L^{2}(\mu_{z})
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}:=\big(\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(\cdot)\otimes\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(\cdot)]+\sigma_{1}\mathfrak{G}_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})}\big)^{-1}(\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(\cdot)\otimes\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot)]).

(A.4)

For any
0<t<1
and any
\mu_{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})
with an optimal transport map
V
from
\mu_{x}
to
\mu_{x}^{\prime}
, there is
W_{2}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x}),(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))=o(t)
.

Proof of
A.5
.

Recall that
\mathscr{F}_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{x})^{2}]+\frac{\zeta_{1}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{z}}[\|z\|^{2}]+\sigma_{1}\mathrm{Ent}(\mu_{z})
.
For a fixed
\mu_{x}
, since the entropy term is geodesically convex
[
79
]
and the mean squared loss term is strongly geodesically convex as
\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(\cdot)\otimes\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(\cdot)]\succeq\Lambda^{2}\mathrm{Id}>0
.
Therefore, for a fixed
\mu_{x}
,
\mathscr{F}_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})
is
\Lambda
-geodesically convex in
\mu_{z}
.

Recall that the Wasserstein gradient of
\mathcal{F}_{1}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})
with respect to
\mu_{z}
, denoted as
\mathcal{G}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z})
, is

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z}):=\left\{z\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}\right)\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]+\zeta_{1}z+\sigma_{1}\nabla\log\mu_{z}(z)\right\}.

(A.5)

From
1
, for any
\mu_{z},\mu_{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}})
and any
\mu_{x}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})
, let
\mathscr{V}
be the optimal transport map from
\mu_{z}
to
\mu_{z}^{\prime}
, then

\displaystyle\Lambda W_{2}^{2}(\mu_{z}^{\prime},\mu_{z})\leq\left\langle\mathcal{G}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\prime})-\mathcal{G}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z}),\mathscr{V}-\mathrm{Id}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu_{z})}.

Consider two probability measures
\mu_{z}^{\ast}((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x})
and
(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})
.
Denote
V_{0}
as the optimal transport map from
(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})
to
\mu_{z}^{\ast}((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x})
.
We have

\displaystyle\quad\Lambda\cdot\|V_{0}-\mathrm{Id}\|_{L^{2}((\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))}=\Lambda\cdot W_{2}^{2}\left(\mu_{z}^{\ast}((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x}),(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left\langle\mathcal{G}\left((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x})\right)-\mathcal{G}\left((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x}),(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)\right.,

\displaystyle\quad\left.V_{0}-\mathrm{Id}\right\rangle_{L^{2}((\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))},

By the optimality condition, we have
\mathcal{G}\left((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x})\right)=0
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\quad\Lambda W_{2}^{2}\left(\mu_{z}^{\ast}((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x}),(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{G}\left((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x}),(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)\|_{L^{2}((\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))}\cdot\|V_{0}-\mathrm{Id}\|_{L^{2}((\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))}

\displaystyle=\|\mathcal{G}\left((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x}),(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)\|_{L^{2}((\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot W_{2}\left(\mu_{z}^{\ast}((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x}),(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right).

So we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\quad\Lambda W_{2}\left(\mu_{z}^{\ast}((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x}),(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)\\
&\leq\|\mathcal{G}\left((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x}),(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)\|_{L^{2}((\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))}.\end{aligned}

(A.6)

Recall the definition of
\mathcal{G}
in Eq. (
A.5
), there is

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{G}\left((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x}),(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)\left(z+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z)\right)

(A.7)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z))\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}(x+tV(x))\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}\right)\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z))\right]

(A.8)

\displaystyle\qquad+\zeta_{1}\left(z+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z)\right)+\sigma_{1}\nabla\log\left((\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)(z+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z)).

(A.9)

Then we have,

(
A.8
)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z))\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\cdot\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z))\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}(x+tV(x))\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}\cdot\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z))\right]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})-\smallint\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}(x)\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}\right)\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z))\right]}_{(\ast)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+t\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\left(\smallint\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z)^{\top}\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{z}-\smallint V(x)^{\top}\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}(x)\;\mathrm{d}\mu_{x}\right)\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)\right]+o(t).

The last equality holds by a Taylor expansion that
\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z))=\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z)^{\top}\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}(z)+o(t)
and
\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}(x+tV(x))=\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}(x)+tV(x)^{\top}\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}(x)+o(t)
.
And we also have,

(
A.9
)

\displaystyle=\zeta_{1}(z+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z))+\sigma_{1}\nabla\log(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z))

\displaystyle=\zeta_{1}(z+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z))+\sigma_{1}\nabla\log\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z))-t\cdot\sigma_{1}\mathbf{H}\log\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z)\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z)

\displaystyle\qquad-t\cdot\sigma_{1}\mathbf{J}\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z)\nabla\log\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z)-t\cdot\sigma_{1}\nabla\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{J}\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z))+o(t)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\zeta_{1}(z+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z))+\sigma_{1}\nabla\log\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})(z+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z))}_{(\ast\ast)}+t\cdot\sigma_{1}\mathfrak{G}_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})}\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z)+o(t).

The second last step holds by
10.6
and the last step holds by the definition of
\mathfrak{G}
.
Since
(\ast)+(\ast\ast)=\mathcal{G}(\mu_{x},\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))(z+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z))=0
from the optimality of
\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})
, we achieve

\displaystyle\quad\eqref{eq:calG_1}+\eqref{eq:calG_2}

\displaystyle=t\cdot\Big(-(\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\otimes\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{a}}])V+(\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}\otimes\nabla\Psi_{\mathbf{w}}]+\sigma_{1}\mathfrak{G}_{\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})})\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V]\Big)+o(t)

\displaystyle=o(t).

The last inequality holds by the definition of
\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}
in Eq. (
A.4
) which kills the linear term in
t
.
Therefore, we have proved that
\mathcal{G}\left((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x}),(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x})\right)\left(z+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V](z)\right)=o(t)
. Hence, putting it back to Eq. (
A.6
), and we have proved that
W_{2}(\mu_{z}^{\ast}((\mathrm{Id}+tV)_{\#}\mu_{x}),(\mathrm{Id}+t\mathfrak{F}_{\mu_{x}}[V])_{\#}\mu_{z}^{\ast}(\mu_{x}))=0(t)
which concludes the proof.
∎
